# AI Media Scout — 2026-11-15

34 complete documentation guides and 36 additional screened open-source creative-AI discoveries. 7 earlier screened entries now have complete guides; these completions are not new applications or launch claims. Concrete uses include local stem separation, reference-led storyboards, lyric timing, editable scene/hair research, personal glyph generation and classical search-based music composition. Every admitted entry has a current complete software-grant review and six explicit quality checks; none was installed or run. Missing model/service rights, independent use and reproduction remain visible. Live primary research supplements all 106 effective fields and all eight configured ecosystems. 182 distinct web targets were captured or attempted; 10 remained failed/access-limited, including Codeberg search throttling, missing pages and TLS/525 errors. Seven complete paper captures were read only through their first two pages, not exhaustively. Broader creative discovery and the complete retained metadata catalog were inspected beyond the automatic README shortlist. Raw/unreviewed catalog records stay local. The weekly review was current for all 2,856 previously published entries at start; current source reviews for every new record and completed guide extend that coverage. Coverage is not quality approval. A pre-alpha tag did not automatically make Filmkit Experimental after its v0.1 epic was found closed. A further current-collection metadata pass inspected 194 potentially relevant records and admitted 20 after fresh source and grant checks; other raw candidates remain unreviewed locally rather than receiving inferred eligibility or quality labels. Planning-only SpeechCraft, deterministic metadata-only recovery and the inspected non-AI IllumiRays features were not admitted. No unchanged complete guides are recycled. No star floor, tool quota, platform preference, app installation, separate scheduler or social publication was used. Verified collection: 932/932 repository queries attempted; 932 successful, 0 partial, 0 failed and 72 bounded/truncated. All 25 model tasks were attempted; 0 failed. These are bounded discovery attempts, not an exhaustive census or quality approval. Empty model-source responses: text2text-generation. An empty response does not establish that these creative workflows have no tools. Separate from successful search queries, 50 watchlist fetches and 6 enrichment attempts failed; the detailed warnings remain in the report. A 404/409 is an availability gap, not proof of abandonment or license revocation.

Documentation reviewed today. Tools are not hands-on tested unless explicitly stated. Requirements and performance remain source-specific.

## Search and review counts

106 creative fields; 932/932 repository queries attempted; 25/25 model-task queries attempted. 66823 distinct source candidates and 1075 model leads. 34 detailed profiles and 36 additional screened discoveries. Source gaps: 0 failed repository queries, 0 partial repository queries, 72 bounded repository queries, 0 failed model-task queries and 0 web ecosystem gaps. Raw search candidates include duplicates of known tools, excluded projects and projects awaiting review; they are not verified recommendations.

## Research beyond GitHub

- **gitlab** · searched: Live topic listings and raw project documentation/grants were revisited for video editing, generation and creative media. Retained JoyAI/openedit are not relisted as new discoveries. Xotion’s inspected grant remains PolyForm Noncommercial, so it is contextual/excluded rather than admitted open-source software. This is bounded source-group research, not a complete host census. [Source](https://gitlab.com/celestialtech/joyai-video-edit-mlx/-/raw/main/README.md) [Source](https://gitlab.com/afsh4ck/openedit/-/raw/main/README.md) [Source](https://gitlab.com/ahkamboh/xotion-studio/-/raw/main/LICENSE) [Source](https://gitlab.com/explore/projects/topics/video-generation?sort=stars_desc)
- **codeberg** · searched: Imaginer and Raspberry Pi diffusion pages and repository metadata were accessible in current captures. Broader diffusion/audio API searches returned HTTP 429, and web-search crawling was robots-blocked. These dated failures limit ecosystem exploration; accessible project pages do not clear every current model/setup dependency. [Source](https://codeberg.org/Imaginer/Imaginer) [Source](https://codeberg.org/MightyOwlbear/Raspberry-Pi-5-Diffusion) [Source](https://codeberg.org/api/v1/repos/Imaginer/Imaginer) [Source](https://codeberg.org/api/v1/repos/search?q=diffusion&limit=25) [Source](https://codeberg.org/api/v1/repos/search?q=audio&limit=25)
- **sourcehut** · searched: The live diffusion project search and diffuser source/license/manifest were revisited. The manifest’s author-local dependency path remains a portability gap. No unchanged retained project is repeated as a new tool, and a project listing is not proof of a usable creative workflow. [Source](https://sr.ht/projects?search=diffusion) [Source](https://git.sr.ht/~rockerboo/diffuser/blob/main/Cargo.toml) [Source](https://git.sr.ht/~rockerboo/diffuser/blob/main/LICENSE)
- **packages** · searched: Live npm, PyPI, NuGet and Rust metadata supplemented discovery without installation. The score-reader package links a released code/model workflow with a separately read full software grant; PyClef’s package listing did not supply the missing complete software grant. Geniartor’s current metadata identifies an AI/artistic package, while its actual source implements classical variable-neighborhood music search rather than a learned model. [Source](https://pypi.org/pypi/score-reader/json) [Source](https://pypi.org/pypi/pyclef/json) [Source](https://pypi.org/pypi/geniartor/json) [Source](https://registry.npmjs.org/litert-ocr/latest) [Source](https://api.nuget.org/v3-flatcontainer/tensorstack.media/index.json) [Source](https://crates.io/api/v1/crates/beatnet-rs)
- **creative-plugins** · searched: REAPER’s Ai Transcriber integration was reviewed for its concrete MuScriptor audio-to-MIDI route, with the proprietary REAPER host and separate models disclosed. Current Krita/SAT/Max-related primary material also supplements host/plugin discovery. A plugin frontend or open wrapper does not license its required host/model. [Source](https://github.com/Aiwist/ai-transcriber) [Source](https://docs.interstice.cloud/) [Source](https://suite.sat.qc.ca/en/tools/) [Source](https://sat-mtl.github.io/koaia/installation.html)
- **project-sites** · searched: Fresh primary captures supplement all 106 effective fields, including book arts and foundation-model dioramas. Named failures include KinoDraw/MapStory/PICT project URLs, openslides TLS and a manimvox 525 response; app shells are not substituted for full manuals. Seven primary PDFs were captured completely and only their first two pages extracted/read, not claimed as full-paper review. [Source](https://3dlg-hcvc.github.io/diorama/) [Source](https://jonidimo.github.io/agenda-heritage-generator/) [Source](https://digital-salon.github.io/) [Source](https://zenodo.org/records/18999747) [Source](https://www.computationalimaging.org/publications/neuralholography/)
- **research-code** · searched: Research-code source review covered scene assembly, hair grooming, human motion, smoke reconstruction, handwriting and symbolic music. Complete software grants are checked independently from paper reputation, pretrained weights and datasets. Diorama’s open asset-preparation reports and Digital Salon’s platform build reports remain unresolved setup evidence; research origin alone does not determine Experimental. [Source](https://github.com/3dlg-hcvc/diorama) [Source](https://github.com/digital-salon/Digital-Salon) [Source](https://github.com/UCSB-AI/EvoScene) [Source](https://github.com/mccuba/FlowMotion) [Source](https://github.com/Xayah-Hina/EPINF-Smoke) [Source](https://github.com/Nikolay-Lysenko/geniartor/blob/master/geniartor/optimization.py)
- **international** · searched: Source review included Chinese glyph/layout and CJK typography guidance, Korean lyric timing, Persian browser vocal separation and Russian/English REAPER transcription. Conventional rendering, optional AI modes and external providers were separated. Documentation language and popularity do not establish licensing, quality or platform support. [Source](https://github.com/zhang-yunqi/TextDiff) [Source](https://github.com/Songzhi-lab/chinese-font-selector) [Source](https://github.com/elecpure2/LyricsSync) [Source](https://github.com/Sajadapp/Vocal-Splitter-Local) [Source](https://github.com/Aiwist/ai-transcriber)

## VocalSplitter Local

Audio, music & voice · Browser tools & web media

First complete guide in this library, including completion of any earlier screened entry; not a claim of a launch today.

### Quality assessment: Quality unverified

Software licensing and a concrete creative AI use are supported. The remaining checks do not establish a recommendation.

Scope: Preparing a rehearsal backing track or isolating a vocal for an authorized remix without uploading the selected audio.

Evidence checked: 2026-11-15. No local execution implied.

- **Open-source software license · verified:** The complete root software license was read and archived. The grant covers this project code; unresolved model, service and asset terms are disclosed separately. [Source](https://github.com/Sajadapp/Vocal-Splitter-Local/blob/main/LICENSE)
- **Creative results · documented:** The README supplies a local-extension workflow and a Persian guide; no independently assessed separation examples were established. [Source](https://github.com/Sajadapp/Vocal-Splitter-Local/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/Sajadapp/Vocal-Splitter-Local/blob/main/package.json)
- **Reproducible setup · documented:** Build the source with npm, enable Chrome extension developer mode, load the dist directory unpacked, and select a short audio file in the popup. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. [Source](https://github.com/Sajadapp/Vocal-Splitter-Local/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/Sajadapp/Vocal-Splitter-Local/blob/main/package.json)
- **Maintenance and support · unknown:** Reviewed up to ten most recently updated issue/PR records; 0 were returned. This bounded sample and the captured release/default-branch record do not establish sustained support, closed blockers or a working complete workflow. [Source](https://github.com/sajadapp/vocal-splitter-local/issues)
- **Independent use · unknown:** Current documentation and the bounded issue/PR evidence were examined. Complete independent first-hand creative use for this scoped workflow was not established; the sample is not an exhaustive user census. [Source](https://github.com/sajadapp/vocal-splitter-local/issues)
- **Models, services and costs · unknown:** The first use downloads an approximately 172 MB HTDemucs ONNX model from timcsy/demucs-web-onnx. The README explicitly says to check model terms before redistribution; a complete checkpoint grant was not established here. The documented workflow has no paid API or audio-upload service; local compute and model download are still needed. Complete model/service/asset clearance remains pending. [Source](https://github.com/Sajadapp/Vocal-Splitter-Local/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/Sajadapp/Vocal-Splitter-Local/blob/main/package.json)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. Keep the popup open. Choirs, live recordings and heavy effects can separate imperfectly. The application code grant does not clear the music or converted model.

### How it uses AI

A Chrome extension runs an HTDemucs ONNX model through demucs-web and ONNX Runtime Web to separate an uploaded song into vocal and instrumental WAV files. [Source](https://github.com/Sajadapp/Vocal-Splitter-Local/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/Sajadapp/Vocal-Splitter-Local/blob/main/package.json)

### Introduction

A Chrome extension runs an HTDemucs ONNX model through demucs-web and ONNX Runtime Web to separate an uploaded song into vocal and instrumental WAV files. [Source 1](https://github.com/Sajadapp/Vocal-Splitter-Local/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/Sajadapp/Vocal-Splitter-Local/blob/main/package.json)

### What it is good for

Preparing a rehearsal backing track or isolating a vocal for an authorized remix without uploading the selected audio. [Source 1](https://github.com/Sajadapp/Vocal-Splitter-Local/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/Sajadapp/Vocal-Splitter-Local/blob/main/package.json)

### Demo & examples

The README supplies a local-extension workflow and a Persian guide; no independently assessed separation examples were established. [Source 1](https://github.com/Sajadapp/Vocal-Splitter-Local/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/Sajadapp/Vocal-Splitter-Local/blob/main/package.json)

### Install

Build the source with npm, enable Chrome extension developer mode, load the dist directory unpacked, and select a short audio file in the popup. [Source 1](https://github.com/Sajadapp/Vocal-Splitter-Local/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/Sajadapp/Vocal-Splitter-Local/blob/main/package.json)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Build the source with npm, enable Chrome extension developer mode, load the dist directory unpacked, and select a short audio file in the popup.

```sh
npm install
```


```sh
npm run build
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/Sajadapp/Vocal-Splitter-Local/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/Sajadapp/Vocal-Splitter-Local/blob/main/package.json)

1. Load dist as an unpacked Chrome extension.
2. Choose an audio file and leave the popup open while the model runs.
3. Compare the vocal and instrumental WAV files against the original, listening for leakage.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/Sajadapp/Vocal-Splitter-Local/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/Sajadapp/Vocal-Splitter-Local/blob/main/package.json)

- **Hardware:** Approximately 172 MB initial model download is documented; minimum RAM, VRAM, CPU class and total working disk are not documented.
- **Software:** Node/npm for the build; Chrome; demucs-web ^1.0.2 and onnxruntime-web ^1.20.1 in package.json. WebGPU with a documented WASM fallback.
- **Platforms:** Chrome extension route; broader browser and operating-system parity is not established by this review.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/Sajadapp/Vocal-Splitter-Local/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/Sajadapp/Vocal-Splitter-Local/blob/main/README.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/Sajadapp/Vocal-Splitter-Local/blob/main/package.json)

- **Code:** MIT
- **Weights:** The first use downloads an approximately 172 MB HTDemucs ONNX model from timcsy/demucs-web-onnx. The README explicitly says to check model terms before redistribution; a complete checkpoint grant was not established here.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows MIT. The first use downloads an approximately 172 MB HTDemucs ONNX model from timcsy/demucs-web-onnx. The README explicitly says to check model terms before redistribution; a complete checkpoint grant was not established here.
- **Cost:** The documented workflow has no paid API or audio-upload service; local compute and model download are still needed.

### Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/Sajadapp/Vocal-Splitter-Local/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/Sajadapp/Vocal-Splitter-Local/blob/main/package.json)

### Limitations

Keep the popup open. Choirs, live recordings and heavy effects can separate imperfectly. The application code grant does not clear the music or converted model. [Source 1](https://github.com/Sajadapp/Vocal-Splitter-Local/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/Sajadapp/Vocal-Splitter-Local/blob/main/package.json)

### Get the tool

- [Repository](https://github.com/Sajadapp/Vocal-Splitter-Local)
- [Documentation](https://github.com/Sajadapp/Vocal-Splitter-Local/blob/main/README.md)

## Stemify Audio Splitter

Audio, music & voice · Editing, captions & post-production

First complete guide in this library, including completion of any earlier screened entry; not a claim of a launch today.

### Quality assessment: Quality unverified

Software licensing and a concrete creative AI use are supported. The remaining checks do not establish a recommendation.

Scope: Auditioning song stems and preparing material for an arrangement in a DAW.

Evidence checked: 2026-11-15. No local execution implied.

- **Open-source software license · verified:** The complete root software license was read and archived. The grant covers this project code; unresolved model, service and asset terms are disclosed separately. [Source](https://github.com/huchukato/stemify-audio-splitter/blob/main/LICENSE)
- **Creative results · documented:** The README shows the app and Ableton configuration. These are maintainer examples, not an independent audio-quality evaluation. [Source](https://github.com/huchukato/stemify-audio-splitter/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/huchukato/stemify-audio-splitter/blob/main/demucs-backend/pyproject.toml)
- **Reproducible setup · documented:** Use a linked desktop release where available, or configure the Python backend and Node frontend from source. The README asks for Python 3.12+, while the backend manifest declares >=3.11,<3.14; this discrepancy is retained. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. [Source](https://github.com/huchukato/stemify-audio-splitter/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/huchukato/stemify-audio-splitter/blob/main/demucs-backend/pyproject.toml)
- **Maintenance and support · documented:** Reviewed up to ten most recently updated issue/PR records; 10 were returned. This bounded sample and the captured release/default-branch record do not establish sustained support, closed blockers or a working complete workflow. [Source](https://github.com/huchukato/stemify-audio-splitter/issues)
- **Independent use · unknown:** Current documentation and the bounded issue/PR evidence were examined. Complete independent first-hand creative use for this scoped workflow was not established; the sample is not an exhaustive user census. [Source](https://github.com/huchukato/stemify-audio-splitter/issues)
- **Models, services and costs · unknown:** Demucs/htdemucs weights and dependencies have separate grants. Ableton Live is a proprietary optional host; the source app MIT grant does not license Ableton, music inputs or every dependency. Local processing; any optional Ableton license and chosen AI client access are separate costs. Complete model/service/asset clearance remains pending. [Source](https://github.com/huchukato/stemify-audio-splitter/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/huchukato/stemify-audio-splitter/blob/main/demucs-backend/pyproject.toml)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. The backend manifest and README disagree on the minimum Python version. Linux desktop packaging and CPU/GPU performance were not verified. Bundled installer automation also configures an Ableton remote script and deserves user review.

### How it uses AI

Stemify combines Demucs v4 stem separation with waveform review, a separation library, and optional Ableton MCP handoff. BPM/key estimation and file packaging are separate conventional processing. [Source](https://github.com/huchukato/stemify-audio-splitter/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/huchukato/stemify-audio-splitter/blob/main/demucs-backend/pyproject.toml)

### Introduction

Stemify combines Demucs v4 stem separation with waveform review, a separation library, and optional Ableton MCP handoff. BPM/key estimation and file packaging are separate conventional processing. [Source 1](https://github.com/huchukato/stemify-audio-splitter/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/huchukato/stemify-audio-splitter/blob/main/demucs-backend/pyproject.toml)

### What it is good for

Auditioning song stems and preparing material for an arrangement in a DAW. [Source 1](https://github.com/huchukato/stemify-audio-splitter/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/huchukato/stemify-audio-splitter/blob/main/demucs-backend/pyproject.toml)

### Demo & examples

The README shows the app and Ableton configuration. These are maintainer examples, not an independent audio-quality evaluation. [Source 1](https://github.com/huchukato/stemify-audio-splitter/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/huchukato/stemify-audio-splitter/blob/main/demucs-backend/pyproject.toml)

### Install

Use a linked desktop release where available, or configure the Python backend and Node frontend from source. The README asks for Python 3.12+, while the backend manifest declares >=3.11,<3.14; this discrepancy is retained. [Source 1](https://github.com/huchukato/stemify-audio-splitter/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/huchukato/stemify-audio-splitter/blob/main/demucs-backend/pyproject.toml)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Use a linked desktop release where available, or configure the Python backend and Node frontend from source. The README asks for Python 3.12+, while the backend manifest declares >=3.11,<3.14; this discrepancy is retained.

```sh
cd demucs-backend
```


```sh
uv sync
```


```sh
uv run python -m gunicorn --bind 0.0.0.0:5001 --workers 1 --timeout 600 app:app
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/huchukato/stemify-audio-splitter/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/huchukato/stemify-audio-splitter/blob/main/demucs-backend/pyproject.toml)

1. Start the backend and frontend using the documented separate-terminal route, or open the desktop build.
2. Drop an MP3 smaller than the documented 20 MB limit.
3. Audition stems before downloading files or a ZIP; optionally arrange reviewed stems in Ableton.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/huchukato/stemify-audio-splitter/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/huchukato/stemify-audio-splitter/blob/main/demucs-backend/pyproject.toml)

- **Hardware:** The README describes an approximately 84 MB model download and Apple MPS with CPU fallback. Minimum RAM, VRAM and total disk are not documented.
- **Software:** Python/uv, PyTorch, Demucs, Flask, librosa and a Node 18+ React frontend. Self-hosted frontend uses npm install then npm run dev in demucs-gui. Ableton MCP is optional.
- **Platforms:** Documented self-hosted Linux/macOS and Windows launchers. Listed release assets cover Apple Silicon macOS and Windows x64; a listed Linux workflow does not establish a Linux release asset.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/huchukato/stemify-audio-splitter/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/huchukato/stemify-audio-splitter/blob/main/README.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/huchukato/stemify-audio-splitter/blob/main/demucs-backend/pyproject.toml)

- **Code:** MIT
- **Weights:** Demucs/htdemucs weights and dependencies have separate grants. Ableton Live is a proprietary optional host; the source app MIT grant does not license Ableton, music inputs or every dependency.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows MIT. Demucs/htdemucs weights and dependencies have separate grants. Ableton Live is a proprietary optional host; the source app MIT grant does not license Ableton, music inputs or every dependency.
- **Cost:** Local processing; any optional Ableton license and chosen AI client access are separate costs.

### Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/huchukato/stemify-audio-splitter/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/huchukato/stemify-audio-splitter/blob/main/demucs-backend/pyproject.toml)

### Limitations

The backend manifest and README disagree on the minimum Python version. Linux desktop packaging and CPU/GPU performance were not verified. Bundled installer automation also configures an Ableton remote script and deserves user review. [Source 1](https://github.com/huchukato/stemify-audio-splitter/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/huchukato/stemify-audio-splitter/blob/main/demucs-backend/pyproject.toml)

conda create -n digital-salon python=3.10

Research demonstrations do not establish universal CAD availability, printable geometry, exact object matches or a one-command creator application. Open reports request CAD/neutral-render assets and a data-preparation guide. One user reports configuring the environment but remains blocked on data preparation; that is not independent end-to-end reproduction.

### How it uses AI

Diorama assembles a compositional CAD scene from one image using open-vocabulary perception, learned depth, shape retrieval, a GPT-powered scene graph and layout optimization. [Source](https://github.com/3dlg-hcvc/diorama/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://3dlg-hcvc.github.io/diorama/)

### Introduction

Diorama assembles a compositional CAD scene from one image using open-vocabulary perception, learned depth, shape retrieval, a GPT-powered scene graph and layout optimization. [Source 1](https://github.com/3dlg-hcvc/diorama/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://3dlg-hcvc.github.io/diorama/)

### What it is good for

Studying an editable scene reconstruction or a text-to-image-to-scene concept rather than a fused surface-only scan. [Source 1](https://github.com/3dlg-hcvc/diorama/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://3dlg-hcvc.github.io/diorama/)

### Demo & examples

The author project page shows synthetic, internet-image and text-to-scene examples. Those examples do not independently verify the current released asset and setup route. [Source 1](https://github.com/3dlg-hcvc/diorama/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://3dlg-hcvc.github.io/diorama/)

### Install

Clone submodules and follow the CUDA-oriented Python 3.10 setup, including SAM2, GroundingDINO, PyTorch3D, FAISS and open_clip_mod. Obtain every required checkpoint and CAD resource before running components. [Source 1](https://github.com/3dlg-hcvc/diorama/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://3dlg-hcvc.github.io/diorama/)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Clone submodules and follow the CUDA-oriented Python 3.10 setup, including SAM2, GroundingDINO, PyTorch3D, FAISS and open_clip_mod. Obtain every required checkpoint and CAD resource before running components.

```sh
git clone --recurse-submodules https://github.com/3dlg-hcvc/diorama.git
```


```sh
conda create -n diorama python=3.10
```


```sh
conda activate diorama
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/3dlg-hcvc/diorama/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://3dlg-hcvc.github.io/diorama/)

1. Prepare one image, required CAD/model assets and the provider key in your own environment.
2. Run object recognition, depth/perception, shape retrieval and pose stages as separate documented run.py operations.
3. Run layout optimization and separately inspect the architecture-reconstruction stages and resulting scene.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/3dlg-hcvc/diorama/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://3dlg-hcvc.github.io/diorama/)

- **Software:** README setup pins torch 2.5.1/torchvision 0.20.1 CUDA 12.1; SAM2, GroundingDINO, xformers, PyTorch3D and faiss-gpu are separate dependencies. The repository uses setup.py; a requested pyproject.toml returned 404.
- **Platforms:** CUDA-oriented shell/Conda instructions; a supported Windows or macOS installation is not documented.

Start with a working ACE-Step installation and set the documented ACESTEP_ROOT/temporary/cache paths for your own machine. Review individual script docstrings; evaluation uses a separate environment. [Source 1](https://github.com/Justinandjohnson/acestep-lora-toolkit/blob/main/README.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Start with a working ACE-Step installation and set the documented ACESTEP_ROOT/temporary/cache paths for your own machine. Review individual script docstrings; evaluation uses a separate environment.
### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/Justinandjohnson/acestep-lora-toolkit/blob/main/README.md)

1. Audit source permissions, clip integrity and caption diversity before training.
2. Use a gated launcher only after dataset and preprocessing tensor counts agree.
3. Compare candidates and run the cover-similarity test; inspect music by ear as well as model scores.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/Justinandjohnson/acestep-lora-toolkit/blob/main/README.md)

- **Hardware:** Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
- **Software:** A working ACE-Step environment; Python scripts, PowerShell launchers, Demucs, CLAP and optional MusicGen routes. No independent universal installation is supplied.
- **Platforms:** PowerShell-oriented launchers are documented; Python-script portability and a full macOS/Linux training route are not established.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/Justinandjohnson/acestep-lora-toolkit/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/Justinandjohnson/acestep-lora-toolkit/blob/main/README.md)

- **Code:** MIT
- **Weights:** MIT covers this surrounding tooling only. ACE-Step, Demucs, CLAP, MusicGen, training music and new adapters have separate rights; no weights or audio are distributed here.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows MIT. MIT covers this surrounding tooling only. ACE-Step, Demucs, CLAP, MusicGen, training music and new adapters have separate rights; no weights or audio are distributed here.
- **Cost:** Local compute plus any chosen external model or service; provider access and charges are separate.

### Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/Justinandjohnson/acestep-lora-toolkit/blob/main/README.md)

### Limitations

build-dataset-v3.py is deliberately deprecated after a real label-collapse failure and should not be copied as the default recipe. CLAP can rank similarity without proving a usable or non-copying result. A complete model/data terms audit remains pending. [Source 1](https://github.com/Justinandjohnson/acestep-lora-toolkit/blob/main/README.md)

### How it uses AI

TextDiff combines a personal handwritten-glyph library with integrated FontDiffuser generation for missing characters and conventional page layout/export. An optional DiffBrush adapter targets whole-line handwriting. [Source](https://github.com/zhang-yunqi/TextDiff/blob/main/README.md)

### Introduction

TextDiff combines a personal handwritten-glyph library with integrated FontDiffuser generation for missing characters and conventional page layout/export. An optional DiffBrush adapter targets whole-line handwriting. [Source 1](https://github.com/zhang-yunqi/TextDiff/blob/main/README.md)

### What it is good for

Creating a proofed handwritten-style article from your own samples while tracking which glyphs were human-written, generated or substituted. [Source 1](https://github.com/zhang-yunqi/TextDiff/blob/main/README.md)

### Demo & examples

The README documents collection, missing-glyph review, shared LoRA adaptation and print-ready PNG/PDF steps. These are documentation claims, not verified handwriting fidelity or print alignment. [Source 1](https://github.com/zhang-yunqi/TextDiff/blob/main/README.md)

### Install

Follow the uv/pyproject source setup, including the fontdiffuser extra, and launch the Qt application. The README clone command contains a placeholder URL; obtain this actual repository instead. Download model weights separately. [Source 1](https://github.com/zhang-yunqi/TextDiff/blob/main/README.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Follow the uv/pyproject source setup, including the fontdiffuser extra, and launch the Qt application. The README clone command contains a placeholder URL; obtain this actual repository instead. Download model weights separately.

```sh
uv sync --extra fontdiffuser
```


```sh
uv run python -m app.main
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/zhang-yunqi/TextDiff/blob/main/README.md)

1. Collect and label your own sample glyphs, keeping provenance explicit.
2. Generate only missing glyphs with FontDiffuser, inspect them and approve each before library admission.
3. Lay out a short page and proof its PDF/PNG at actual size; leave unresolved glyphs blank if appropriate.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/zhang-yunqi/TextDiff/blob/main/README.md)

- **Hardware:** README recommends 8+ GB RAM for generation/fine-tuning and approximately 400 MB FontDiffuser weights; minimum VRAM and complete disk footprint are not documented.
- **Software:** Python 3.10+ (3.12 recommended by the README), uv, PySide6, PyTorch/diffusers and FontDiffuser weights. Optional OCR is not yet wired into the GUI. DiffBrush needs a separate CUDA/Conda setup.
- **Platforms:** README documents macOS/Linux/Windows for the main application. Its optional DiffBrush route explicitly excludes Apple Silicon/CPU and uses CUDA-distributed inference; this limitation is not applied to the entire app.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/zhang-yunqi/TextDiff/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/zhang-yunqi/TextDiff/blob/main/README.md)

- **Code:** MIT
- **Weights:** The project code grant is reviewed separately below. FontDiffuser, optional DiffBrush/SD1.5, source fonts, shared LoRA data and generated handwriting require their own terms and consent review.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows MIT. The project code grant is reviewed separately below. FontDiffuser, optional DiffBrush/SD1.5, source fonts, shared LoRA data and generated handwriting require their own terms and consent review.
- **Cost:** Local compute plus any chosen external model or service; provider access and charges are separate.

### Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/zhang-yunqi/TextDiff/blob/main/README.md)

### Limitations

Generated handwriting is not an authentic handwritten original. Optional OCR is unfinished; DiffBrush is separate from the primary glyph workflow. A local Gradio interface sends one structured batch request through Conductor Core to an LLM provider and presents four editable/downloadable MIDI loop variants. [Source 1](https://github.com/laceyp99/conductor-studio/blob/main/README.md)

2. Use Python 3.10+ and uv 0.12.6+, synchronize the project, start conductor-studio and privately configure one provider or a reachable Ollama service.

```

```

A native SwiftUI studio connects separately installed ACE-Step or a paid GPTunneL/Suno route to song generation, editing and a conventional Python DSP mixing/mastering engine. [Source](https://github.com/MRX7999/AI-Music-Studio/blob/main/README.md)

### Introduction

A native SwiftUI studio connects separately installed ACE-Step or a paid GPTunneL/Suno route to song generation, editing and a conventional Python DSP mixing/mastering engine. [Source 1](https://github.com/MRX7999/AI-Music-Studio/blob/main/README.md)

### What it is good for

Developing a generated song sketch, arranging stems in a virtual room and preparing a manually reviewed release package. [Source 1](https://github.com/MRX7999/AI-Music-Studio/blob/main/README.md)

### Demo & examples

The README documents app screenshots and measured DSP tests. Its offline procedural demo is explicitly not AI; passing DSP or mocked-provider tests does not verify music-generation quality. [Source 1](https://github.com/MRX7999/AI-Music-Studio/blob/main/README.md)

### Install

Use the Apple Silicon release on macOS 14+, supply Python 3.10–3.12 and engine requirements, then separately configure ACE-Step or the cloud fallback. Intel Macs have a documented source-build option. [Source 1](https://github.com/MRX7999/AI-Music-Studio/blob/main/README.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Use the Apple Silicon release on macOS 14+, supply Python 3.10–3.12 and engine requirements, then separately configure ACE-Step or the cloud fallback. Intel Macs have a documented source-build option.

```


```sh
macos/build_app.sh
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/MRX7999/AI-Music-Studio/blob/main/README.md)

1. Verify the engine/interpreter and start a small demo session to learn the UI.
2. Connect the separately running ACE-Step server or deliberately configure the paid cloud route for actual AI generation.

### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/MRX7999/AI-Music-Studio/blob/main/README.md)

- **Hardware:** Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
- **Software:** macOS 14+, Python 3.10–3.12; Swift/Xcode or Command Line Tools for builds. Optional FFmpeg for ALAC/MP3. ACE-Step is installed/started separately; heavy separation/restoration models are optional.
- **Platforms:** Native release: Apple Silicon macOS. Intel source build is documented. A legacy PyQt/CLI route exists, but native feature parity across other operating systems is not asserted.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/MRX7999/AI-Music-Studio/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/MRX7999/AI-Music-Studio/blob/main/README.md)

- **Code:** MIT
- **Weights:** MIT covers project code. ACE-Step, GPTunneL/Suno, Demucs, restoration/voice models and optional tools have separate terms. The README identifies Matchering as GPL and not bundled; no complete combined rights clearance is claimed.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows MIT. MIT covers project code. ACE-Step, GPTunneL/Suno, Demucs, restoration/voice models and optional tools have separate terms. The README identifies Matchering as GPL and not bundled; no complete combined rights clearance is claimed.
- **Cost:** Local model/compute cost or separately paid GPTunneL/Suno usage; optional publishing accounts and checks are separate. No automatic publishing is performed by this research review.

### Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/MRX7999/AI-Music-Studio/blob/main/README.md)

### Limitations

The virtual microphones are generic curves, not measured branded profiles; room modeling is not acoustic ray tracing. Built-in HPSS produces pseudo-stems; clean learned vocal separation requires a separate model. Release builds are not notarized. [Source 1](https://github.com/MRX7999/AI-Music-Studio/blob/main/README.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Install the documented score-reader package with the desired PDF/audio extras under Python 3.10 or 3.11, and provide Java 17+ on PATH or through JAVA_HOME. Run the built-in demo before an owned score.

```sh
python -m pip install "score-reader[pdf,audio]"
```


```sh
score-reader --demo -o scale.json --musicxml scale.musicxml --midi scale.mid --mp3 scale.mp3
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/Luckolite/Sheet-Music-Recognition/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/Luckolite/Sheet-Music-Recognition/blob/main/docs/licensing.md)

1. Inspect the demo event JSON and score against the supplied input.
2. Convert one authorized score image or selected PDF pages, choosing fallback key/meter/tempo deliberately.
3. Correct recognized notes, rhythm, repeats and expression in a notation editor before trusting playback or publishing the result.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/Luckolite/Sheet-Music-Recognition/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/Luckolite/Sheet-Music-Recognition/blob/main/docs/licensing.md)

- **Hardware:** Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
- **Software:** Python 3.10/3.11, Java 17+, bundled recognition model and separately installed runtime dependencies. Audio export needs FFmpeg supplied by the audio extra. Optional ONNX/Java adapters are separate routes.
- **Platforms:** A Python/Java route and a separate Android demo are documented. Platform adapter availability does not establish identical model/features on every device.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/Luckolite/Sheet-Music-Recognition/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/Luckolite/Sheet-Music-Recognition/blob/main/README.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/Luckolite/Sheet-Music-Recognition/blob/main/docs/licensing.md)

- **Weights:** The complete root grant is Apache-2.0. docs/licensing.md explicitly includes original code, synthetic examples and listed model weights, while keeping fonts, optional Verovio and runtime components under separate terms. Input-score rights remain the user’s responsibility.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows Apache-2.0. The complete root grant is Apache-2.0. docs/licensing.md explicitly includes original code, synthetic examples and listed model weights, while keeping fonts, optional Verovio and runtime components under separate terms. Input-score rights remain the user’s responsibility.

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/Luckolite/Sheet-Music-Recognition/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/Luckolite/Sheet-Music-Recognition/blob/main/docs/licensing.md)

### Limitations

MusicXML reconstructs an editable concert-pitch score rather than preserving the original page layout or tab placement. Missing guitar-tab rhythm is estimated. Model accuracy, independent reproduction and the complete runtime distribution notices need review. ### How it uses AI

An agent skill interprets a floor-plan sketch into explicit/estimated/inferred dimensions, requires human reconciliation, then uses the separate CADCraft MCP backend for vector drafting and checks. [Source](https://github.com/hasnizihar/sketch-to-cad/blob/main/README.md)

### Introduction

An agent skill interprets a floor-plan sketch into explicit/estimated/inferred dimensions, requires human reconciliation, then uses the separate CADCraft MCP backend for vector drafting and checks. [Source 1](https://github.com/hasnizihar/sketch-to-cad/blob/main/README.md)

### What it is good for

Preparing a preliminary editable single-storey orthogonal floor-plan draft while preserving measurement uncertainty. [Source 1](https://github.com/hasnizihar/sketch-to-cad/blob/main/README.md)

### Demo & examples

The README documents a seven-stage example and delivery artifacts. Its acceptance report is a workflow artifact, not independent architectural or engineering certification. [Source 1](https://github.com/hasnizihar/sketch-to-cad/blob/main/README.md)

On the documented Windows route, install the Antigravity environment and CADCraft CLI v0.8.0+ separately. Review the PowerShell skill installer and configure the CADCraft MCP executable path in the target workspace. [Source 1](https://github.com/hasnizihar/sketch-to-cad/blob/main/README.md)

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/hasnizihar/sketch-to-cad/blob/main/README.md)

- **Models, services and costs · unknown:** MIT covers this project code. There are no learned model weights in this search-based route; synthesis, notation dependencies, any fonts/sounds and musical source material retain separate terms. Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. This is heuristic computational composition, not learned imitation or a neural music generator. The primary package is classified as AI/artistic software and the inspected source implements the stated search; reproducibility and the complete output dependency chain remain unverified. An open configuration report shows KeyError for a scale identifier named minor. This is a user-reported configuration incompatibility, not proof that all minor scales are unsupported; use the source-defined identifiers and verify a changed configuration.

### How it uses AI

Geniartor is a classical search-based music composer: variable-neighborhood search improves a piece against user-weighted musical criteria, with perturbation when progress reaches a local optimum. It does not use an LLM or neural checkpoint. [Source](https://github.com/Nikolay-Lysenko/geniartor/blob/master/README.md) [Source](https://pypi.org/project/geniartor/) [Source](https://github.com/Nikolay-Lysenko/geniartor/blob/master/geniartor/optimization.py)

First launch creates a Python environment, installs the ACE-Step requirements and downloads models; keep the server console open while the browser UI is running. [Source 1](https://github.com/dmhagar/candy-dungeon-music-forge/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/dmhagar/candy-dungeon-music-forge/blob/main/TRADEMARKS.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. The documented user route is the official Windows installer. First launch creates a Python environment, installs the ACE-Step requirements and downloads models; keep the server console open while the browser UI is running.
### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/dmhagar/candy-dungeon-music-forge/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/dmhagar/candy-dungeon-music-forge/blob/main/TRADEMARKS.md)

1. Generate a short instrumental or supply owned lyrics with section markers.
2. Inspect saved tracks and presets; apply optional stem separation only after selecting a candidate.
3. For training, provide matching audio, prompt and lyric files, select adapter settings and inspect a small run before a larger dataset.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/dmhagar/candy-dungeon-music-forge/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/dmhagar/candy-dungeon-music-forge/blob/main/TRADEMARKS.md)

- **Hardware:** Author minimum: NVIDIA GPU, approximately 10–12 GB VRAM and SSD with tens of GB free. Comfortable configuration: 12–24 GB VRAM and 32 GB RAM. These are documented estimates, not measurements from this review.
- **Software:** Windows installer; Python/PyTorch and ACE-Step environment created on first launch. Optional audio-separator and MuFun-ACEStep add separate downloads.
- **Platforms:** Windows 10/11 64-bit and NVIDIA RTX-oriented instructions. No Mac/Linux workstation route is established by this review.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/dmhagar/candy-dungeon-music-forge/blob/main/LICENSE.txt) [Source 2](https://github.com/dmhagar/candy-dungeon-music-forge/blob/main/README.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/dmhagar/candy-dungeon-music-forge/blob/main/TRADEMARKS.md)

- **Code:** Apache-2.0
- **Weights:** Apache-2.0 covers source code. ACE-Step, audio-separator, MuFun, datasets and other checkpoints retain separate grants. The complete TRADEMARKS.md was read; names/logos are reserved separately from software rights.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows Apache-2.0. Apache-2.0 covers source code. ACE-Step, audio-separator, MuFun, datasets and other checkpoints retain separate grants. The complete TRADEMARKS.md was read; names/logos are reserved separately from software rights.
- **Cost:** Local GPU computation, storage and model downloads; no blanket clearance for music, lyrics, samples or model redistribution.

### Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/dmhagar/candy-dungeon-music-forge/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/dmhagar/candy-dungeon-music-forge/blob/main/TRADEMARKS.md)

### Limitations

An optional MuFun analyzer is experimental and can generate incorrect labels; that optional feature alone does not label the whole workstation Experimental. Reproducibility, model permissions and independent creative use remain unverified. An open first-hand support report says the default setup does not work on Blackwell RTX 50-series/CUDA 13.x, and describes a local wheel change as a workaround. This review did not reproduce or establish an upstream shipped fix; the README hardware range is not a universal GPU compatibility guarantee. [Source 1](https://github.com/dmhagar/candy-dungeon-music-forge/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/dmhagar/candy-dungeon-music-forge/blob/main/TRADEMARKS.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/dmhagar/candy-dungeon-music-forge/issues/2)

### Get the tool

- [Documentation](https://github.com/dmhagar/candy-dungeon-music-forge/blob/main/README.md)

Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. - **Models, services and costs · unknown:** GPL-3.0 covers the code. REAPER is a separately licensed proprietary DAW, ADSR has separate terms, and the experimental sample datasets are not distributed because of copyright. Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. The current README says retrieval is not fully robust and may fail to match clips or disrupt harmonic coherence; instrument assignment remains manual and the code is not fully tested. Mixing, sound design and parameter automation are outside its supported scope.

### How it uses AI

An LLM arranges retrieved MIDI/audio clips into full-song sections, tracks and transformations, then loads that structured composition into REAPER for human editing. [Source](https://github.com/heqi201255/TOMI/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/heqi201255/TOMI/blob/main/requirements.txt)

An LLM arranges retrieved MIDI/audio clips into full-song sections, tracks and transformations, then loads that structured composition into REAPER for human editing. Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. The validator is itself a model and may miss errors. Preview-model availability can change; repeated regeneration adds cost. The advertised prompt-sanitization/bypass behavior was not enabled or tested in this review.

### How it uses AI

Gemini text/image models analyze story scenes, create character/location references, generate illustrations and run a model-based validation/regeneration loop. [Source](https://github.com/artryazanov/ai-illustrator/blob/main/README.md)

### Introduction

Gemini text/image models analyze story scenes, create character/location references, generate illustrations and run a model-based validation/regeneration loop. [Source 1](https://github.com/artryazanov/ai-illustrator/blob/main/README.md)

### What it is good for

Preparing a reference-linked sequence of story illustrations for manual art-direction review. [Source 1](https://github.com/artryazanov/ai-illustrator/blob/main/README.md)

### Demo & examples

A Kolobok showcase supplies maintainer character and scene images. Automated judge scores, mocked tests and structured output do not establish reliable character/anatomical consistency. [Source 1](https://github.com/artryazanov/ai-illustrator/blob/main/README.md)

### Install

Provide Python 3.12+ or the documented Docker route, install requirements, and privately configure a Google API key with access to compatible text/validator/image models. [Source 1](https://github.com/artryazanov/ai-illustrator/blob/main/README.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Provide Python 3.12+ or the documented Docker route, install requirements, and privately configure a Google API key with access to compatible text/validator/image models.

```sh
git clone https://github.com/artryazanov/ai-illustrator.git
```


```sh
pip install -r requirements.txt
```


```sh
python main.py --text-file data/my_story.txt --output-dir output/my_project_name
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/artryazanov/ai-illustrator/blob/main/README.md)

1. Prepare an authorized story and select model, aspect ratio and style settings.
2. Generate a short sequence and compare character/location references against every scene.
3. Review regeneration decisions, correct visual defects and inspect the data.json manifest before publishing illustrations.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/artryazanov/ai-illustrator/blob/main/README.md)

- **Hardware:** Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
- **Software:** Python 3.12+, Google GenAI client/runtime requirements and optional Docker/Compose. Access to the configured image-generation model is required.
- **Platforms:** Local Python and Docker routes are documented; the reviewed README does not provide a tested OS support matrix.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/artryazanov/ai-illustrator/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/artryazanov/ai-illustrator/blob/main/README.md)

- **Code:** MIT
- **Weights:** MIT covers software. Gemini service/model access, story text, references and generated assets remain separate; API-based image generation is not an offline model.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows MIT. MIT covers software. Gemini service/model access, story text, references and generated assets remain separate; API-based image generation is not an offline model.
- **Cost:** Google model requests and regeneration can incur separate charges; no current price or quota guarantee was verified.

### Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/artryazanov/ai-illustrator/blob/main/README.md)

### Limitations

The validator is itself a model and may miss errors. Preview-model availability can change; repeated regeneration adds cost. The advertised prompt-sanitization/bypass behavior was not enabled or tested in this review. [Source 1](https://github.com/artryazanov/ai-illustrator/blob/main/README.md)

Timing preservation, language accuracy and accessibility need review; the README describes possible future DeepSeek support rather than an established alternate route. An open issue warns that translations for longer films can exceed response limits and produce empty captions. The title is a reported limitation, not an execution test by this project.

### Conductor Core · MIT

Adding prompt-to-MIDI generation to a creative notebook, CLI or composition interface.
A reusable engine assembles musical prompts, invokes hosted or Ollama LLMs, validates structured four-bar loops and converts them to MIDI with artifact history.
Full setup, hardware, model-term and output-quality review pending. The current API is pre-release and consumers are told to pin a tag. Model/provider terms and optional FluidSynth/FFmpeg/SoundFonts are separate. Structured MIDI validation does not verify musical merit or independent reproduction. Current support evidence reports absolute paths in persisted artifact metadata, with a proposed fix still open. Inspect exported metadata before sharing it and do not assume the patch is shipped.

### Stroke2Font Pipeline · MIT

Studying the implementation of a handwriting-to-font research scaffold before attempting font production.
The source implements a style-conditioned neural diffusion scaffold, preprocessing and font export pipeline; its current default constructs a new model and warns when no pretrained checkpoint is supplied.
Full setup, hardware, model-term and output-quality review pending. The actual pipeline falls back to random initialization without a model path, and a complete trained checkpoint/conditioning route was not established. README claims of ready few-shot font production are not validated. Training data, font rights and current setup remain pending.

### Quality assessment: Experimental

Software license and creative AI relevance screened; full guide and quality checks remain pending.

Scope: Studying the implementation of a handwriting-to-font research scaffold before attempting font production.

Evidence checked: 2026-11-15. No local execution implied.

Why Experimental: The current pipeline explicitly uses random initialization when no pretrained model is supplied, while the inspected generator does not use the passed character-content features to condition its denoising beyond batch shape. The shipped default does not establish the advertised trained character-specific font generator. https://github.com/metrovoc/stroke2font-pipeline/blob/master/README.md https://github.com/metrovoc/stroke2font-pipeline/blob/main/src/pipeline.py https://github.com/metrovoc/stroke2font-pipeline/blob/main/src/style_transfer.py

### AutoDJ AI · MIT

Experimenting with explainable transitions across a locally owned music set.
A browser DJ prototype combines conventional harmonic/beat scoring and Web Audio playback with optional Demucs neural stem separation to detect vocal overlap.
Full setup, hardware, model-term and output-quality review pending. Neural vocal analysis is disabled by default; the fallback is spectral estimation. ### AI PDF TOC Bookmarker · MIT

Adding a reviewed navigable contents structure to an authorized scanned book.
A vision-and-tool-capable external agent reads rendered scanned contents pages, structures a hierarchy and uses PyMuPDF helpers to write PDF bookmarks.
Full setup, hardware, model-term and output-quality review pending. The agent must support both vision and tool execution; deterministic rendering/bookmark scripts alone are not AI inference. ### 3D filmmaking multimodal evals · GPL-3.0

Studying explicit evaluation failures in a consent-based single-presenter/light-field workflow.
An avatar/voice generation and evaluation pipeline combines learned matting and monocular depth with a conventional 77-view light-field warp and scoped quality probes.
Full setup, hardware, model-term and output-quality review pending. The current README discloses three fail-open gates, ungated synchrony/gesture problems and failed level/lip-sync checks in its showcased render. Its unattended/advertising-grade claims do not clear those gaps. Vendor access, voice consent, model rights and Apple Silicon setup need further review.

### treemation · MIT

Drafting a locally editable vertical educational video from a topic and supplied narration.
An external coding agent plans an Indonesian-language narrated 3D explainer and authors scenes, while a Three.js/Chromium pipeline renders a pixel-style host, captions and sound.
Full setup, hardware, model-term and output-quality review pending. Narration is generated separately through ElevenLabs or supplied by the user. Default silence/punctuation timing is heuristic; optional Whisper adds a separate model. Agent access, font/asset rights and independent output quality remain pending; rendering is not enabled by this research.

## Excluded and unresolved findings

Research notes only; these do not enter the eligible tool library.

- **VisionCaptioner software grant** · needs-license-review: The complete root text differs from standard Apache-2.0 in the Legal Entity definition and attribution wording, in addition to capitalization. Recognized metadata alone does not resolve the operative wording. The current project is held pending a clarified recognized software grant. [Source](https://github.com/Brekel/VisionCaptioner/blob/main/LICENSE)
- **Heritage Studio / YunType / MCDCNFD / PyClef** · needs-license-review: Promising book-layout, font and music workflows were located, but the inspected license endpoints returned 404 and their inspected root listings did not supply a complete software-license file. README/package claims or public code alone are insufficient. These findings do not assert that no grant could exist elsewhere; admission remains pending complete evidence. [Source](https://github.com/jonidimo/agenda-heritage-generator) [Source](https://github.com/yuanbw2025/yuntype) [Source](https://github.com/uraniceye/MCDCNFD) [Source](https://github.com/viniciusfs14/PyClef)
- **NeuroFont** · excluded: The current root listing contains LICENSE and README.md only, while model architecture, dataset, weights and inference/export implementation are unchecked roadmap items. A licensed proposal is not an available creative AI application. [Source](https://github.com/Cosebe/NeuroFont)
- **Xotion Studio** · excluded: The inspected complete project grant remains PolyForm Noncommercial. Creative relevance and a public repository do not meet the open-source software eligibility rule under this grant. [Source](https://gitlab.com/ahkamboh/xotion-studio/-/raw/main/LICENSE)
- **ZONOS3-Mini full weight grant** · needs-license-review: The current model card and publisher page are accessible, while the requested complete LICENSE returns 404. The card’s label is not a substitute for reading the complete grant. [Source](https://huggingface.co/Zyphra/ZONOS3-Mini/raw/main/README.md) [Source](https://huggingface.co/Zyphra/ZONOS3-Mini/raw/main/LICENSE)
